Book 1, Chapter 15

Kandu, Mārīṣā, and Dakṣa’s Creation

VP 1.15.1

तपश् चरत्सु पृथिवीं प्रचेतःसु महीरुहाः ।
अरक्ष्यमाणाम् आवव्रुः बभूवाथ प्रजाक्षयः ॥१॥

When they were deeply immersed in penance like this, the entire world was filled by trees, growing by great proportions. Mankind declined, and nobody could survive because the plants completely filled the world.

No commentary.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa
तपश् — austerities
चरत्सु — while practicing
पृथिवीं — the earth
प्रचेतःसु — among the Pracetases
महीरुहाः — trees
अरक्ष्यमाणाम् — unprotected
आवव्रुः — they covered
बभूवाथ — then came to be
प्रजाक्षयः — a decline of people
Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

tapaś caratsu pṛthivīṃ pracetaḥsu mahīruhāḥ | arakṣyamāṇām āvavruḥ babhūvātha prajākṣayaḥ || 1 ||

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

तपश् चरत्सु पृथिवीं प्रचेतःसु महीरुहाः ।
अरक्ष्यमाणाम् आवव्रुः बभूवाथ प्रजाक्षयः ॥१॥

VP 1.15.2

नाशकन् मारुतो वातुं वृतं खम् अभवद् द्रुमैः ।
दशवर्षसहस्राणि न शेकुश् चेष्टितुं प्रजाः ॥२॥

The trees went up to the sky, and air was not able to blow at all. People were not able to move around or do any functioning, for ten thousand years.

No commentary.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa
नाशकन् — were unable
मारुतो — the wind
वातुं — to blow
वृतं — enclosed
खम् — sky
अभवद् — became
द्रुमैः — by trees
दशवर्षसहस्राणि — ten thousand years
न — not
शेकुश् — they were able
चेष्टितुं — to move
प्रजाः — the people
Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

nāśakan māruto vātuṃ vṛtaṃ kham abhavad drumaiḥ | daśavarṣasahasrāṇi na śekuś ceṣṭituṃ prajāḥ || 2 ||

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

नाशकन् मारुतो वातुं वृतं खम् अभवद् द्रुमैः ।
दशवर्षसहस्राणि न शेकुश् चेष्टितुं प्रजाः ॥२॥

VP 1.15.3

तान् दृष्ट्वा जलनिष्क्रान्ताः सर्वे क्रुद्धाः प्रचेतसः ।
मुखेभ्यो वायुम् अग्निं च तेऽसृजन् जातमन्यवः ॥३॥

Seeing such a sorry state in the world, the Prachetasaas got very angry, and becoming very angry, they blew air and fire through their mouth.

Śrīdhara translation

When the Pracetases were engaged in penance, their father had obtained knowledge of the truth, abandoned the kingdom, and gone to the forest; this should be understood. As the Bhāgavata says, Nārada compassionately instructed Prācīnabarhis, whose mind was attached to ritual works, through the story of Purañjana. Thus, because the earth was unprotected and there was no cultivation, the trees covered it.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa
तान् — those
दृष्ट्वा — having seen
जलनिष्क्रान्ताः — risen from water
सर्वे — all
क्रुद्धाः — enraged
प्रचेतसः — the Pracetases
मुखेभ्यो — from their mouths
वायुम् — wind
अग्निं — fire
च — and
तेऽसृजन् — they sent forth
जातमन्यवः — with anger arisen
Śrīdhara
प्रचेतःसु — among the Pracetases
तपश् — austerity
चरत्सु — among those moving
तत्पितरि — in his father
च — and
नादात् — did not give
तत्त्वज्ञानं — knowledge of reality
प्राप्य — having obtained
राज्यं — kingdom
त्यक्त्वा — having abandoned
वनं — forest
गते — when gone
सति — when; being
इति — thus
द्रष्टव्यम् — is to be understood
तद् — that
उक्तम् — it is stated
प्राचीनबर्हिषं — Prācīnabarhis
क्षत्तकर्मस्व् in the duties of a Kṣatriya
आसक्तचेतसम् — whose mind is attached
नारदोऽध्यात्मतत्त्वज्ञः — Nārada, knower of the truth of the self
कृपालुः — compassionate
प्रत्यबोधयत् — he instructed; awakened
भा.पु — Bhāgavata Purāṇa
इत्य्आदिना — by the phrase beginning “son of Diti”
पुरञ्जनाख्यानेन — through the account of Purañjana
अतोऽरक्ष्यमाणां — therefore, the unprotected one
पृथिवीं — the Earth
कर्षणाद्य्अभावाद् — because ploughing and the like were absent
महीरुहा — trees
आवृतवन्तः — they covered
Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

tān dṛṣṭvā jalaniṣkrāntāḥ sarve kruddhāḥ pracetasaḥ | mukhebhyo vāyum agniṃ ca te’sṛjan jātamanyavaḥ || 3 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

pracetaḥsu tapaś caratsu tatpitari ca nādāt tattvajñānaṃ prāpya rājyaṃ tyaktvā vanaṃ gate sati iti draṣṭavyam| tad uktam— prācīnabarhiṣaṃ kṣattakarmasv āsaktacetasam| nārado’dhyātmatattvajñaḥ kṛpāluḥ pratyabodhayat || [bhā.pu. 4.25.3] ityādinā purañjanākhyānena| ato’rakṣyamāṇāṃ pṛthivīṃ karṣaṇādyabhāvād mahīruhā āvṛtavantaḥ

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

प्रचेतःसु तपश् चरत्सु तत्पितरि च नादात् तत्त्वज्ञानं प्राप्य राज्यं त्यक्त्वा वनं गते सति इति द्रष्टव्यम्। तद् उक्तम्— प्राचीनबर्हिषं क्षत्तकर्मस्व् आसक्तचेतसम्। नारदोऽध्यात्मतत्त्वज्ञः कृपालुः प्रत्यबोधयत् ॥ [भा.पु. ४.२५.३] इत्य्आदिना पुरञ्जनाख्यानेन। अतोऽरक्ष्यमाणां पृथिवीं कर्षणाद्य्अभावाद् महीरुहा आवृतवन्तः

VP 1.15.4

उन्मूलान् अथ तान् वृक्षान् कृत्वा वायुर् अशोषयत् ।
तान् अग्निर् अदहद् घोरस् तत्राभूद् द्रुमसङ्क्षयः ॥४॥

The air that they blew was so powerful that it uprooted all the trees, and the air dried everything. The fire which they blew burnt everything. Then, there was a severe lack of trees.

Śrīdhara translation

He explains the purpose of the creation of air and fire: “they uprooted.”

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa
उन्मूलान् — uprooted
अथ — then
तान् — those
वृक्षान् — trees
कृत्वा — having made
वायुर् — the wind
अशोषयत् — dried
तान् — them
अग्निर् — the fire
अदहद् — burned
घोरस् — fierce
तत्राभूद् — thereupon came
द्रुमसङ्क्षयः — the destruction of trees
Śrīdhara
वायोर् — the rudiment of touch
अग्नेः — of Agni; of fire
सृष्टेः — of creation
प्रयोजनम् — purpose
आह — he says
उन्मूलान् — uprooted
इति — thus
Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

unmūlān atha tān vṛkṣān kṛtvā vāyur aśoṣayat | tān agnir adahad ghoras tatrābhūd drumasaṅkṣayaḥ || 4 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

vāyor agneḥ sṛṣṭeḥ prayojanam āha—unmūlān iti

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

वायोर् अग्नेः सृष्टेः प्रयोजनम् आह—उन्मूलान् इति

VP 1.15.5

द्रुमक्षयम् अथो दृष्ट्वा किञ्चिच् छिष्टेषु शाखिषु ।
उपागम्याब्रवीद् एतान् राजा सोमः प्रजापतीन् ॥५॥

King Soma, having seen the destruction of the trees and that only a few branches remained, approached the Prajāpatis and spoke.

Śrīdhara translation

Soma is the king of trees. By saying “Prajāpatis” he indicates a conciliatory approach: it is improper for you, protectors of creatures, to burn trees that nourish creatures with roots, fruits and fruits.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa
द्रुमक्षयम् — destruction of trees
अथो — then
दृष्ट्वा — having seen
किञ्चिच् — a little
छिष्टेषु — remaining
शाखिषु — among branches
उपागम्याब्रवीद् — approaching, he spoke
एतान् — to these
राजा — the king
सोमः — Soma
प्रजापतीन् — to the Prajāpatis
Śrīdhara
सोमो — fire
वृक्षाणां — of trees
राजा — the king
प्रजापतीन् — to the Prajāpatis
इत्य् — thus
अनेन — by this
कन्दमूलफलादिभिः — with bulbs, roots, fruits, and the like
प्रजावृद्धिकारिणां — of those who increase offspring
वृक्षाणां — of trees
दाहः — dāhaḥ
प्रजापालकानां of the protectors of the subjects
युष्माकम् — of you
अनुचितः — anucitaḥ
इति — thus
सामोपायः — sāmopāyaḥ
.सूचितः — sūcitaḥ
Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

drumakṣayam atho dṛṣṭvā kiñcic chiṣṭeṣu śākhiṣu | upāgamyābravīd etān rājā somaḥ prajāpatīn || 5 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

somo vṛkṣāṇāṃ rājā| prajāpatīn ity anena "kandamūlaphalādibhiḥ prajāvṛddhikāriṇāṃ vṛkṣāṇāṃ dāhaḥ prajāpālakānāṃ yuṣmākam anucitaḥ" iti sāmopāyaḥ .sūcitaḥ

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

सोमो वृक्षाणां राजा। प्रजापतीन् इत्य् अनेन "कन्दमूलफलादिभिः प्रजावृद्धिकारिणां वृक्षाणां दाहः प्रजापालकानां युष्माकम् अनुचितः" इति सामोपायः .सूचितः

VP 1.15.6

कोपं यच्छत राजानः शृणुध्वं च वचो मम ।
सन्धानं वः करिष्यामि सह क्षितिरुहैर् अहम् ॥६॥

O great kings, please do control your anger and listen to me. I will make sure that trees and plants are not going to cause more trouble to you. I will make sure that they live in peace with you.

Śrīdhara translation

“Control your anger” means restrain it.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa
कोपं — anger
यच्छत — restrain!
राजानः — O kings
शृणुध्वं — listen!
च — and
वचो — words
मम — my
सन्धानं — reconciliation
वः — for you
करिष्यामि — I shall make
सह — together with
क्षितिरुहैर् — trees
अहम् — I
Śrīdhara
कोपं — anger
यच्छत — restrain!
नियमयत restrain; regulate
Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

kopaṃ yacchata rājānaḥ śṛṇudhvaṃ ca vaco mama | sandhānaṃ vaḥ kariṣyāmi saha kṣitiruhair aham || 6 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

kopaṃ yacchata niyamayata

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

कोपं यच्छत नियमयत

VP 1.15.7

रत्नभूता च कन्येयं वार्क्षेयी वरवर्णिनी ।
भविष्यज्जानता पूर्वं मया गोभिर् विवर्धिता ॥७॥

I also have this wonderful woman, gem of a woman, one who is born of the trees. I am protecting this gem of a woman, excellent among women. Through my grace, I have nurtured her, knowing the future what is going to happen.

Śrīdhara translation

He reconciles them by offering penance: “ratna-bhūtā” means precious; “Vārkṣeyī” means born from trees. “She will be your wife; Dakṣa will be born from her, and the three worlds will be filled by his lineage”—not knowing this, I nourished her with my nectar-filled rays.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa
रत्नभूता — jewel-like
च — and
कन्येयं — this maiden
वार्क्षेयी — born of trees
वरवर्णिनी — excellent in beauty
भविष्यज्जानता — knowing the future
पूर्वं — beforehand
मया — by me
गोभिर् — with rays
विवर्धिता — nurtured
Śrīdhara
तपः — austerity
प्रदानेन — through the granting of
सन्धानं — reconciliation
करोति--रत्नभूता — performs + jewel-like
इति — thus
वार्क्षेयी — born of trees
वृक्षजा born from a tree
भविष्यत — will be
इयं — this (feminine)
युष्माकं — to you all
पत्नी — wife
भविष्यति — will be
अस्यां — of this
दक्षो — Dakṣa
जनिष्यते will be born
तद्वंशेन — tadvaṃśena
च — and
त्रिलोकी the three worlds
पूरयिष्यते will fill
इत्य्आदिर् — and so on
अजानता — he knew
मया — by me
गोभिः — with cows
सुधामयै filled with nectar
रश्मिभिः with rays
संवर्धिता — were increased
Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

ratnabhūtā ca kanyeyaṃ vārkṣeyī varavarṇinī | bhaviṣyajjānatā pūrvaṃ mayā gobhir vivardhitā || 7 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

tapaḥpradānena sandhānaṃ karoti--ratnabhūtā iti| vārkṣeyī vṛkṣajā| bhaviṣyata "iyaṃ yuṣmākaṃ patnī bhaviṣyati, asyāṃ dakṣo janiṣyate, tadvaṃśena ca trilokī pūrayiṣyate" ityādir ajānatā mayā gobhiḥ sudhāmayai raśmibhiḥ saṃvardhitā

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

तपःप्रदानेन सन्धानं करोति--रत्नभूता इति। वार्क्षेयी वृक्षजा। भविष्यत "इयं युष्माकं पत्नी भविष्यति, अस्यां दक्षो जनिष्यते, तद्वंशेन च त्रिलोकी पूरयिष्यते" इत्य्आदिर् अजानता मया गोभिः सुधामयै रश्मिभिः संवर्धिता

VP 1.15.8

मारिषा नाम नाम्नैषा वृक्षाणाम् इति निर्मिता ।
भार्या वोऽस्तु महाभागा ध्रुवं वंशविवर्धिनी ॥८॥

She is the daughter of the trees, vrukshaas, and is called as Maarishaa. She will become your wife and will continue the progeny, and help you in expanding the mankind.

Śrīdhara translation

Her name is Māriṣā; the name means “this girl of the trees.” Thus “created” means produced, as is well known.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa
मारिषा — Mārīṣā
नाम — by name
नाम्नैषा — this one
वृक्षाणाम् — of the trees
इति — thus
निर्मिता — formed
भार्या — wife
वोऽस्तु — yours shall she be
महाभागा — greatly blessed
ध्रुवं — surely
वंशविवर्धिनी — increasing the lineage
Śrīdhara
नाम्ना — by name
मारिषा — Mārīṣā
वृक्षाणाम् — of trees
एषा — this
कन्या — daughters
इति — thus
नाम — named
एवं — thus
हि — indeed
प्रसिद्ध — in common usage
निर्मिता — formed
निष्पन्ना — when completed
इत्य् — thus
अन्वयः — the syntactic connection
Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

māriṣā nāma nāmnaiṣā vṛkṣāṇām iti nirmitā | bhāryā vo’stu mahābhāgā dhruvaṃ vaṃśavivardhinī || 8 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

nāmnā māriṣā vṛkṣāṇām eṣā kanyā iti nāma| evaṃ hi prasiddha nirmitā niṣpannā ity anvayaḥ

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

नाम्ना मारिषा वृक्षाणाम् एषा कन्या इति नाम। एवं हि प्रसिद्ध निर्मिता निष्पन्ना इत्य् अन्वयः

VP 1.15.9

युष्माकं तेजसोऽर्धेन मम चार्धेन तेजसः ।
अस्याम् उत्पत्स्यते विद्वान् दक्षो नाम प्रजापतिः ॥९॥

With half of your tejas, and half of my tejas, in her, one Prajaapati is going to be born, by name Dakṣa.

Śrīdhara translation

With half of your intense, ascetic radiance and half of my radiance, joined in her—she having grown by my cool rays—Dakṣa will be born.

Viṣṇu Purāṇa

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

yuṣmākaṃ tejaso’rdhena mama cārdhena tejasaḥ | asyām utpatsyate vidvān dakṣo nāma prajāpatiḥ || 9 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

yuṣmākaṃ tapomayasya tīvrasya tejaso’rdhena śītair madīyair gobhiḥ saṃvṛddhatvād asyā mama ca tejaso’rdhena saṃyukto’syām utpatsyate

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

युष्माकं तपोमयस्य तीव्रस्य तेजसोऽर्धेन शीतैर् मदीयैर् गोभिः संवृद्धत्वाद् अस्या मम च तेजसोऽर्धेन संयुक्तोऽस्याम् उत्पत्स्यते

VP 1.15.10

मम चांशेन संयुक्तो युष्मत्तेजोमयेन वै ।
तेजसाग्रिसमो भूयः प्रजाः संवर्धयिष्यति ॥१०॥

Dakṣa having my amsha, and also your tejas, both, Maarishaa and Prachetasaas, and Maarishaa was nurtured by Soma also, Dakṣa will have all qualities like fire, veerya, shourya, and will also have all pleasing qualities, which are Soma's gunaas, and he will help in expanding the mankind.

Śrīdhara translation

Thus, joined with my gentle lunar portion and the fire consisting of your radiance, he will be of the nature of fire and moon; becoming equal to fire and invincible, he will increase the creatures.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

mama cāṃśena saṃyukto yuṣmattejomayena vai | tejasāgrisamo bhūyaḥ prajāḥ saṃvardhayiṣyati || 10 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

evaṃ ca saumyena mama aṃśena yuṣmattejomayena cāgninā saṃyukto’gniṣomātmakaḥ san agnisamaḥ apradhṛṣyo bhūtvā prajāḥ saṃvarddhayiṣyatīty arthaḥ

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

एवं च सौम्येन मम अंशेन युष्मत्तेजोमयेन चाग्निना संयुक्तोऽग्निषोमात्मकः सन् अग्निसमः अप्रधृष्यो भूत्वा प्रजाः संवर्द्धयिष्यतीत्य् अर्थः

VP 1.15.11

कण्डुर् नाम मुनिः पूर्वम् आसीद् वेदविदां वरः ।
सुरम्ये गोमतीतीरे स तेपे परमं तपः ॥११॥

There was a rishi called Kandu, well versed in the Vedaas, on the pleasant banks of Gomati river, he was performing tapas. Now, in order to tell the Prachetasaas that Maarishaa is born in a good vamsha, he began to tell about the story of Kandu Maharshi.

Śrīdhara translation

One should not object: how can one wife belong to ten? She was born from Kaṇḍu’s ascetic seed in the womb of an apsaras, was of great power, a goddess and not womb-born, not human. There is no contradiction in one apsaras being enjoyed by many; because of Viṣṇu’s grace her many husbands are not blameworthy. Intending to narrate her birth and related matters, he begins “Kaṇḍu named…” and continues through “your wife, daughter of a king.”

Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

kaṇḍur nāma muniḥ pūrvam āsīd vedavidāṃ varaḥ | suramye gomatītīre sa tepe paramaṃ tapaḥ || 11 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

na caivaṃ śaṅkanīyaṃ daśānāṃ katham eko bhāryā syād iti, yasmād iyaṃ kaṇḍos tapaḥ saṃsṛtena vīryeṇa apsarogarbhasambhūtā mahāprabhāvā ca ayonijā ca devī, na tu mānuṣī| na tu bahūnām ekāpsaraḥsambhogo virudhyate, viṣṇoḥ prasādāt cāsyā bahavaḥ patayo na duṣyantīti varṇayiṣyan tasyā janmādikaṃ vaktum āha—kaṇḍur nāma ityādinā yuṣmatpatnī nṛpātmajā [72] ity antena

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

न चैवं शङ्कनीयं दशानां कथम् एको भार्या स्याद् इति, यस्माद् इयं कण्डोस् तपः संसृतेन वीर्येण अप्सरोगर्भसम्भूता महाप्रभावा च अयोनिजा च देवी, न तु मानुषी। न तु बहूनाम् एकाप्सरःसम्भोगो विरुध्यते, विष्णोः प्रसादात् चास्या बहवः पतयो न दुष्यन्तीति वर्णयिष्यन् तस्या जन्मादिकं वक्तुम् आह—कण्डुर् नाम इत्य्आदिना युष्मत्पत्नी नृपात्मजा [७२] इत्य् अन्तेन

VP 1.15.12

तत्क्षोभाय सुरेन्द्रेण प्रम्लोचाख्या वराप्सराः ।
प्रयुक्ता क्षोभयामास तम् ऋषिं सा शुचिस्मिता ॥१२॥

In order to disturb his penance, Indra sends the apsaras Pramlochaa. She started to disturb his tapas.

Śrīdhara translation

For causing him agitation and destroying his penance through disturbance of mind.

VP 1.15.13

He gets attracted to her, and tapas will be broken. He lives with her more than hundred years, in Mandaraachala, enjoying her company.

No commentary.

VP 1.15.14

प्रसादसुमुखो ब्रह्मन्न् अनुज्ञां दातुम् अर्हसि ॥१४॥

After hundred plus years, she tells Kandu Maharshi, that 'I desire to go back to Deva Loka, be pleased and do allow me to return to Deva Loka '.

No commentary.

punar gate varṣaśate sādhike sā śubhānanā | yāmīty āha divaṃ brahman praṇayasmitaśobhanam || 18 ||

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

पुनर् गते वर्षशते साधिके सा शुभानना ।
यामीत्य् आह दिवं ब्रह्मन् प्रणयस्मितशोभनम् ॥१८॥

VP 1.15.19

उक्तस् तयैवं स मुनिर् उपगुह्यायतेक्षणाम् ।
इहास्यतां क्षणं सुभ्रु चिरकालं गमिष्यसि ॥१९॥

Being told like that, he embraced her and told her 'Please stay for a little more while, because you are going to go away for a long time'.

Śrīdhara translation

He said it while embracing her; this indicates an excess of intercourse and thus an excess of affection. “You will be gone for a long time” means that once gone she would not return quickly.

सुभ्रु — O beautiful-browed one
चिरकालं — after a long time
गमिष्यसि — you will go

VP 1.15.23

तया च रमतस् तस्य परमर्षेर् अहर्निशम् ।
नवं नवम् अभूत् प्रेम मन्मथाविष्टचेतसः ॥२३॥

Staying with her, and enjoying her company, day by day, his interest in her, and passion goes on increasing.

Śrīdhara translation

Dakṣiṇā is she who, even while attached elsewhere, does not abandon reverence, fear, love and good will toward the former beloved; that nature is called dākṣiṇya, and by it she went.

VP 1.15.26

ततः प्रहस्य सुदती तं सा प्राह महामुनिम् ।

Smilingly she says 'What, today your day has ended'. She makes fun of him.

Śrīdhara translation

He laughs in astonishment, addressing her reproachfully as “knower of all dharmas”: “What has happened to your day today?”

VP 1.15.27

बहूनां विप्र वर्षाणां परिवृत्तम् अहस् तव ।
गतम् एतन् न कुरुते विस्मयं कस्य कथ्यताम् ॥२७॥

Many years have gone. When you are telling like this, who does not get surprised.

Śrīdhara translation

He himself makes the cause of his astonishment clear: “many” means that the day has undergone transformation over many years; your extremely long day—who would not be amazed? Let this be explained.

Śrīdhara translation

“Sad-bhāva” means the ultimate reality.

Śrīdhara translation

Because of the offense of neglecting his duties, the frightened brāhmaṇa asked her: “What time is it? How much time has passed?”

VP 1.15.33

सत्यं भीरु वदस्य् एतत् परिहासोऽथ वा शुभे ।
दिनम् एकम् अहं मन्ये त्वया सार्धम् इहासितम् ॥३३॥

Are you telling the truth, or are you simply making fun of me? I feel as though I have just spent a day with you.

Śrīdhara translation

“I stayed here” means I remained here.

Devanagari

इहासितम् अत्र स्थितम्

VP 1.15.34

वदिष्याम्यनृतं ब्रह्मन् कथम् अत्र तवान्तिके ।
विशेषेणाद्य भवता पृष्टा मार्गानुवर्तिना ॥३४॥

Pramlochaa say - O great sage, how can I tell a lie to you when I am with you? Especially today, when you are wanting to perform your duties, and going on the path of dharma, how can I tell you a lie?

Śrīdhara translation

How could I now speak falsely, after being worthy of such complete trust for all this time? She was questioned again by one troubled by fear that his duties had been neglected and who followed the righteous path.

VP 1.15.35

निशम्य तद्वचः सत्यं स मुनिर् नृपनन्दनाः ।
धिग् धिङ् माम् इत्यतीवेत्थं निनिन्दात्मानम् आत्मना ॥३५॥

Seeing that what she is telling is true, the Muni says, feeling very bad that such a thing happened - 'Shame on me'.

Śrīdhara translation

“Fie, fie on me” expresses self-reproach; dhik is an indeclinable, and takes the accusative in this construction.

VP 1.15.36

तपांसि मम नष्टानि हतं ब्रह्मविदां धनम् ।
हतो विवेकः केनापि योषिन्मोहाय निर्मिता ॥३६॥

All my tapas is destroyed. Brahman, the wealth of the Brahmavits - knowers of Brahman is lost for me. My viveka is destroyed. Someone has created woman only for delusion.

Śrīdhara translation

“Brahmavidām” means “of those who know the Veda”; “my knowledge was destroyed” means it was lost.

VP 1.15.37

ऊर्मिषट्कातिगं ब्रह्म ज्ञेयम् आत्मजयेन मे ।
मतिर् एषा हृता येन धिक् तं कामं महाग्रहम् ॥३७॥

That Brahman who can be known by realizing the self, crossing over the oormi shatkaas. I wanted to cross over the oormi shatkaas and wanted to realize Brahman through realizing the self. That is destroyed. By kaama, that is destroyed.

Śrīdhara translation

The six waves are hunger, thirst, grief, delusion, old age and death. He had transcended those six waves. “Brahman is to be known by me through conquering the mind”—the understanding formed in this way was stolen from him; that is what he means.

VP 1.15.38

व्रतानि वेदवेद्याप्तिकारणान्यखिलानि च ।
नरकग्राममार्गेण सङ्गेनापहृतानि मे ॥३८॥

The Brahman who is known from the Vedaas, to realize that Brahman, whatever vrataas have to be performed, all those things, I who am going on the path to hell, because of attachment, they are all destroyed. Vrataani vedavedyaaptikaaranaani apahrutaani – means the vratas necessary to attain Brahman who is known from the Vedas is lost for me.

No commentary.

VP 1.15.40

गच्छ पापे यथाकामं यत् कार्यं तत् कृतं त्वया ।
देवराजस्य मत्क्षोभं कुर्वन्त्या भावचेष्टितैः ॥४०॥

O bad woman, go away as you desire. Whatever Indra, Devaraaja had sent you to do, that you have done, you have done your duty. With all your charms, you have attracted me and destroyed my tapas.

Śrīdhara translation

“Bhāva” means love; by the affectionate gestures within it—smiles, frowns of the brows and so on—while disturbing me, you have accomplished the task of the king of the gods.

VP 1.15.41

सतां सप्तपदं मैत्रम् उषितोऽहं त्वया सह ॥४१॥

I am not going to burn you to ashes with the fire of my great anger. For sajjanaas, one following seven steps will become friend. I have lived with you for many hundreds of years.

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

VP 1.15.42

अथवा तव को दोषः किं वा कुप्याम्यहं तव ।
ममैव दोषो नितरां येनाहम् अजितेन्द्रियः ॥४२॥

Or, what is your fault in this? Why should I get angry with you? It is all my fault only that I am not controlling my senses.

Śrīdhara translation

A friendship is conventionally established by walking seven steps together. Yet I have lived with you for a long time; therefore I will not reduce you to ashes with the fire of a curse.

Śrīdhara · transliteration

mārge saptapadāni saha gacchatāṃ maitraṃ prasiddham| ahaṃ punas tvayā bahukālam uṣitaḥ sthitaḥ| atas tvāṃ śāpāgninā nāhaṃ bhasmīkariṣyāmi

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

VP 1.15.43

यया शक्रप्रियार्थिन्या कृतो मे तपसो व्ययः ।
त्वया धिक् तां महामोहमञ्जूषं सुजुगुप्सिताम् ॥४३॥

In order to please Indra, you destroyed my tapas. You are like a box of great delusion.

Śrīdhara translation

Even though his senses were uncontrolled and his mind disturbed, her return occurred for a reason; therefore he rebukes her again: “By you my penance was wasted through intercourse and the like; fie on you, a box containing great delusion.”

VP 1.15.44

यावद् इत्थं स विप्रर्षिस् तां ब्रवीति सुमध्यमाम् ।
तावद् गलत्स्वेदजला साबभूवातिवेपथुः ॥४४॥

As Kandu Maharshi started to show his anger, and was telling like this, she started shivering all over, and had drops of perspiration all over her body.

Śrīdhara translation

“Her sweat-water was dripping” means that drops of perspiration fell from her; she became exceedingly tremulous.

VP 1.15.45

प्रवेपमानां सततं स्विन्नगात्रलतां सतीम् ।
गच्छ गच्छेति सक्रोधम् उवाच मुनिसत्तमः ॥४५॥

She was trembling with fear, and was perspiring all over. The rishi with great anger, said 'Go away, go away'.

Śrīdhara translation

“Her body itself was a creeper” because of softness and similar qualities; he speaks of her whose bodily creeper was wet with sweat.

VP 1.15.46

सा तु निर्भर्त्सिता तेन विनिष्क्रम्य तदाश्रमात् ।
आकाशगामिनी स्वेदं ममार्ज तरुपल्लवैः ॥४६॥

Being sent off like that, she went out of the hermitage. She started to go on her way to Deva Loka. She wiped her sweat with leaves of the plants.

Śrīdhara translation

Having explained that her pregnancy arose from intercourse with Kaṇḍu, that the embryo came out without a passage through the womb, and that perspiration issued forth, he now explains through six verses why the girl was born from trees and was a tree-born girl: “But she, being reproached…”

VP 1.15.49

VP 1.15.52

स चापि भगवान् कण्डुः क्षीणे तपसि सत्तमः ।
पुरुषोत्तमाख्यं मैत्रेय विष्णोर् आयतनं ययौ ॥५२॥

That Kandu, when all his tapas got destroyed, went to Viṣṇu's abode called Purushottama.

Śrīdhara translation

To prevent the disdain that she was the daughter of one fallen from yoga, he again describes Kaṇḍu’s yogic splendour, beginning “and he too,” in two and a half verses.

VP 1.15.53

तत्रैकाग्रमतिर् भूत्वा चकाराराधनं हरेः ।
ब्रह्मपारमयं कुर्वन् जपम् एकाग्रमानसः ।
ऊर्ध्वबाहुर् महायोगी स्थित्वासौ भूपनन्दनाः ॥५३॥

He focussed on Paramaatman, did Viṣṇu's worship there, doing japa on Parabrahman's svaroopa, he, with concentrated mind on Paramaatman, did tapas. Having raised his arms, this great yogi, in standing posture, did tapas on Paramaatman, Viṣṇu.

Śrīdhara translation

The words brahman and pāra have a special sense: “brahma-pāramayam” means filled with or consisting of abundant Brahman. Or, the far shore of Brahman is Vedānta, so “made of that” means having become its very substance.

आराध्यत is worshipped

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

brahmapāraṃ muneḥ śrotum icchāmaḥ paramaṃ stavam | japatā kaṇḍunā devo yenārādhyata keśavaḥ || 54 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

ārādhyata ārādhitaḥ

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

आराध्यत आराधितः

VP 1.15.55

पारं परं विष्णुर् अपारपारपारः
परः परेभ्यः परमार्थरूपी ।
स ब्रह्मपारः परपारभूतः
परः पराणाम् अपि पारपारः ॥५५॥

Viṣṇu is the supreme limit, beyond all limits, higher than the highest, the form of the highest reality; He is the limit of Brahman, the end of all, and beyond all others.

Śrīdhara translation

Having proclaimed the truth in three verses and offered a prayer in the fourth, Hari was quickly pleased with Kaṇḍu through this hymn to the far shore of Brahman.

Śrīdhara · transliteration

saṅkīrtya tattvaṃ triślokyā prārthanā ca caturthataḥ| brahmapārastavenaivaṃ kaṇḍos tuṣṭo’cirād dhariḥ ||

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

सङ्कीर्त्य तत्त्वं त्रिश्लोक्या प्रार्थना च चतुर्थतः। ब्रह्मपारस्तवेनैवं कण्डोस् तुष्टोऽचिराद् धरिः ॥

VP 1.15.56

स कारणं कारणतस् ततोऽपि
तस्यापि हेतुः परहेतुहेतुः ।
कार्येषु चैवं सह कर्मकर्तृ
रूपैर् अशेषैर् अवतीह सर्वम् ॥५६॥

He is the cause, and beyond even causality; He is the cause of that cause. In all effects He is present in every form—as the action and as the agent.

Śrīdhara translation

Having praised him in his pure form, he praises him as all things: “He is the cause.” The suffix -tas has the sense of the sixth case. Viṣṇu is the cause of the cause, and of that cause as well—the chain of causes from the universe to the primal cause. Likewise in effects, Viṣṇu is the chain of effects from prakṛti and mahat to the final effect. Having stated his all-pervasiveness as material cause and effect in creation, he states it in preservation as what is protected and as protector. “He alone” is emphatic: Viṣṇu, marked by all his forms as the objects and agents of protection, sustains everything. “Karma” here is the most desired result, from human joy up to Brahman-bliss; thus Viṣṇu alone is identified with all causes, effects, actions and agents connected with creation and dissolution.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

sa kāraṇaṃ kāraṇatas tato’pi tasyāpi hetuḥ parahetuhetuḥ | kāryeṣu caivaṃ saha karmakartṛ rūpair aśeṣair avatīha sarvam || 56 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

tad evaṃ viśuddharūpeṇa stutvā sārvātmyena stauti—sa kāraṇam iti| kāraṇata iti ṣaṣṭhyarthe tasil| sa viṣṇuḥ kāraṇasyāpi kāraṇaṃ, tasyāpi kāraṇam ity evaṃ carācarakāraṇaṃ brahmāṇḍam ārabhya mūlakāraṇaparyantaṃ kāraṇamālātmakaḥ| yathoktaṃ brahmastutau—kāraṇaṃ kāraṇasyāpi tasya kāraṇam ityādi| kāryeṣu caivam ity anena prakṛtikāryaṃ mahattattvam ārabhya caramakāryaparyantaṃ kāryamālātmako viṣṇur evety uktam|tad evaṃ sṛṣṭikriyāyām upādānakāraṇatvena tatkāryatvena ca sārvātmyam uktvā pālanakriyāyāṃ pālyarūpakarmatayā tatkartṛtayā vā sārvātmyam āha—sa iti| nipāto’vadhāraṇārthaḥ| sa viṣṇur eva dharmādirūpaiḥ pālanīyais tatpālakaiś ca kartṛrūpair aśeṣaiḥ svīyair upalakṣitaḥ san sarvam avatīti| yad vā, aśeṣaiḥ karmarūpaiḥ kartṛrūpaiś ca| sahaivam iti karmakartṛrūpamālāntarātmakaś cātideśaḥ| tatra kartur īpsitatamaṃ karma iti smṛteḥ| kartuḥ kriyayā grāmagamanādikaraṇikayā bhāvanākhyayā yad īpsitatamaṃ sukhaṃ, tad eva mukhyaṃ karm atra grḥyate| grāmādes tu dhātvarthakarmataiva na bhāvanākarmatā| ata evāste śete ityādiṣu akarmakeṣv api pratyayāṃśe bhāvanāpratīter bhāvyaṃ vinā bhāvanābhāvāc ca| sukhakāma āsīt svārthyakāmaḥ śayītyety evam ādiprayogeṣv āsanena sukhaṃ bhāvayed ity evam ādivacanavyaktyā sukhāder bhāvanākarmatvam abhyupagamyate| tad uktam bhaṭṭaiḥ— asty ādāv api kartraṃśe bhāvyam astye va bhāvanā| anyatrāṃśe ca nipātaḥ na tathā sā prabhāsate || iti| yady evam akarmakeṣv api bhāvyaṃ syāt, kas tarhi sakarmakākarmayor bheda ity ākṣipya parihāraś coktaḥ| sākṣād avyabhicāreṇa dhātvartho yatra karmabhāk| sakarmakaḥ sa dhātuḥ syāt pāramparye tv akarmakaḥ || iti| atroktakarmaśabdena īpsitatamasukhavācinā mānuṣānandam ārabhya brahmānandaparyantapuruṣārthasādhako’pi viṣṇur evety uktam| tad evaṃ kāraṇakāryakarmakartṛrūpaiḥ sṛṣṭilayakriyāpratiyogibhis tattatsambandhisārvātmyam āha—aśeṣair etai rūpaiḥ sa viṣṇuḥ sarvam avatīti

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

तद् एवं विशुद्धरूपेण स्तुत्वा सार्वात्म्येन स्तौति—स कारणम् इति। कारणत इति षष्ठ्य्अर्थे तसिल्। स विष्णुः कारणस्यापि कारणं, तस्यापि कारणम् इत्य् एवं चराचरकारणं ब्रह्माण्डम् आरभ्य मूलकारणपर्यन्तं कारणमालात्मकः। यथोक्तं ब्रह्मस्तुतौ—कारणं कारणस्यापि तस्य कारणम् इत्य्आदि। कार्येषु चैवम् इत्य् अनेन प्रकृतिकार्यं महत्तत्त्वम् आरभ्य चरमकार्यपर्यन्तं कार्यमालात्मको विष्णुर् एवेत्य् उक्तम्।तद् एवं सृष्टिक्रियायाम् उपादानकारणत्वेन तत्कार्यत्वेन च सार्वात्म्यम् उक्त्वा पालनक्रियायां पाल्यरूपकर्मतया तत्कर्तृतया वा सार्वात्म्यम् आह—स इति। निपातोऽवधारणार्थः। स विष्णुर् एव धर्मादिरूपैः पालनीयैस् तत्पालकैश् च कर्तृरूपैर् अशेषैः स्वीयैर् उपलक्षितः सन् सर्वम् अवतीति। यद् वा, अशेषैः कर्मरूपैः कर्तृरूपैश् च। सहैवम् इति कर्मकर्तृरूपमालान्तरात्मकश् चातिदेशः। तत्र कर्तुर् ईप्सिततमं कर्म इति स्मृतेः। कर्तुः क्रियया ग्रामगमनादिकरणिकया भावनाख्यया यद् ईप्सिततमं सुखं, तद् एव मुख्यं कर्म् अत्र ग्र्ःयते। ग्रामादेस् तु धात्व्अर्थकर्मतैव न भावनाकर्मता। अत एवास्ते शेते इत्य्आदिषु अकर्मकेष्व् अपि प्रत्ययांशे भावनाप्रतीतेर् भाव्यं विना भावनाभावाच् च। सुखकाम आसीत् स्वार्थ्यकामः शयीत्येत्य् एवम् आदिप्रयोगेष्व् आसनेन सुखं भावयेद् इत्य् एवम् आदिवचनव्यक्त्या सुखादेर् भावनाकर्मत्वम् अभ्युपगम्यते। तद् उक्तम् भट्टैः— अस्त्य् आदाव् अपि कर्त्र्अंशे भाव्यम् अस्त्ये व भावना। अन्यत्रांशे च निपातः न तथा सा प्रभासते ॥ इति। यद्य् एवम् अकर्मकेष्व् अपि भाव्यं स्यात्, कस् तर्हि सकर्मकाकर्मयोर् भेद इत्य् आक्षिप्य परिहारश् चोक्तः। साक्षाद् अव्यभिचारेण धात्व्अर्थो यत्र कर्मभाक्। सकर्मकः स धातुः स्यात् पारम्पर्ये त्व् अकर्मकः ॥ इति। अत्रोक्तकर्मशब्देन ईप्सिततमसुखवाचिना मानुषानन्दम् आरभ्य ब्रह्मानन्दपर्यन्तपुरुषार्थसाधकोऽपि विष्णुर् एवेत्य् उक्तम्। तद् एवं कारणकार्यकर्मकर्तृरूपैः सृष्टिलयक्रियाप्रतियोगिभिस् तत्तत्सम्बन्धिसार्वात्म्यम् आह—अशेषैर् एतै रूपैः स विष्णुः सर्वम् अवतीति

VP 1.15.57

ब्रह्म प्रभुर् ब्रह्म स सर्वभूतो
ब्रह्म प्रजानां पतिर् अच्युतोऽसौ
ब्रह्माक्षरं नित्यम् अजं स विष्णुर्
अपक्षयाद्यैर् अखिलैर् असङ्गि ॥५७॥

He is Brahman, the Lord; He is Brahman, present in all beings; He is Brahman, the imperishable, eternal, unborn Viṣṇu, untouched by decay and all such changes.

Śrīdhara translation

If he is pure Brahman, how can he exist in all forms? If all things, how can he remain pure amid every change? In answer: Viṣṇu is pure Brahman, Lord and controller of all, all beings, and protector of creatures. He is called Acyuta because he never departs from Brahman. Though all-pervading and the Self of all, he remains imperishable, eternal, unborn and free from decay, change, growth or attachment; through his inconceivable self-dependent māyā he appears as all. Thus there is no contradiction.

brahma prabhur brahma sa sarvabhūto brahma prajānāṃ patir acyuto’sau brahmākṣaraṃ nityam ajaṃ sa viṣṇur apakṣayādyair akhilair asaṅgi || 57 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

nanu yadi śuddhaṃ brahmaivāsau viṣṇuḥ, tarhi katham aśeṣai rūpair avatīty ucyate? atha sarvātmakas tarhi sarvabhāvavikāraprāpteḥ kuto’sya viśuddhatā| ity āśaṅkyāha—brahmeti| sa viṣṇuḥ śuddhaṃ brahmaiva san prabhuḥ sarvaniyantā brahmaiva san sarvabhūtaś ca| brahmaiva san prajānāṃ patiś ca pālakaḥ| nanu brahmasvarūpāpracyutyā yato’sāv acyutaḥ prasiddhaḥ| ataḥ sa viṣṇuḥ vyāpanaśīlaḥ| sarvātmako’pi san akṣaraṃ dhruvaṃ nityam ajam apakṣayādyair asaṅgi aparikṣayavipariṇāmavṛddhisaṅgarahitaṃ ca brahmaiva acintyasvādhīnamāyayā sarvātmatayā sa vivartate, ato nāyaṃ virodhaḥ iti bhāvaḥ

VP 1.15.58

ब्रह्माक्षरम् अजं नित्यं यथासौ पुरुषोत्तमः ।
तथा रागादयो दोषा प्रयान्तु प्रशमं मम ॥५८॥

May the faults such as attachment subside in me, as Brahman, the imperishable and unborn, is the Supreme Person.

Śrīdhara translation

Remembering the truth just stated, he prays for the removal of faults: just as the Supreme Person is immutable Brahman, so by remembering him and receiving the revelation of one’s nature through his grace, may all my faults—such as attachment and the defects that cause interruption of rites, failure of yoga and the like—be completely destroyed at the root.

VP 1.15.59

एतद् ब्रह्मपराख्यं वै संस्तवं परमं जपन् ।

By reciting this supreme hymn to Brahman, he attained the highest perfection after worshiping Keśava.

VP 1.15.60

इयं च मारिषा पूर्वम् आसीद् या तां ब्रवीमि वः ।
कार्यगौरवम् एतस्याः कथने फलदायि वः ॥६०॥

This is Mārīṣā, whose story has great significance; hearing it bears fruit for you.

Śrīdhara translation

Now he says “and this…” to state that Bhagavān himself graciously granted her many husbands; neither she nor you are at fault. Her great purpose is obtaining a good son; when this is stated, it means your lineage will be expanded.

Viṣṇu, being worshiped by her, appeared before her and said, “Choose a boon.” She then asked for what she desired.

Śrīdhara translation

She was the king’s wife in a previous birth. When that husband died, she pleased Viṣṇu.

VP 1.15.63

भगवन् बालवैधव्यं वृथाजन्माहम् ईदृशी ।

O Lord, I have been born unfortunate, with a wasted life and the misfortune of widowhood in childhood.

Śrīdhara translation

One whose birth was fruitless is fruitless-born; she was also childless and her birth had no result.

श्लाघ्याः प्रख्यातकर्माणः

VP 1.15.65

कुलं शीलं वयः सत्यं दाक्षिण्यं क्षिप्त्रकारिता ।
अविसंवादिता सत्त्वं वृद्धसेवा कृतज्ञता ॥६५॥

May I be endowed with family, character, age, truthfulness, kindness, promptness, integrity, courage, service to elders, and gratitude.

No commentary.

VP 1.15.66

रूपसम्पत्समायुक्ता सर्वस्य प्रियदर्शना ।
अयोनिजा च जायेयं त्वत्प्रसादाद् अधोक्षज ॥६६॥

May I be beautiful, beloved by all, and born without a womb, by Your grace, O Adhokṣaja.

Śrīdhara translation

Endowed with beauty and prosperity.

प्रणामेण नम्राम्

VP 1.15.68

भविष्यन्ति महावीर्या एकस्मिन्न् एव जन्मनि ।
प्रख्यातोदारकर्माणो भवत्याः पतयो दश ॥६८॥

You shall have ten husbands in a single birth, mighty men renowned for their noble deeds.

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

प्रख्यातोदारकर्माणो भवत्याः पतयो दश ॥६८॥

VP 1.15.69

पुत्रं च सुमहावीर्यं महाबलपराक्रमम् ।
प्रजापतिगुणैर् युक्तं त्वम् अवाप्स्यसि शोभने ॥६९॥

O beautiful one, you shall obtain a son endowed with the qualities of a Prajāpati, greatly heroic and powerful.

Śrīdhara translation

Though, because of separate births, he had granted her request for many husbands, Viṣṇu, unwilling that his devotees should undergo repeated birth, granted that boon in a single birth. Hence the statement: “in one intervening birth.”

VP 1.15.70

He will be the founder of a lineage, and his offspring will fill the three worlds.

Sūti means offspring or continuity.

साध्वी पतिव्रता

VP 1.15.72

इति उक्त्वा अन्तर्दधे देवस् तां विशालविलोचनाम् ।
सा च इयम् मारिषा जाता युष्मत् पत्नी नृपात्मजाः ॥७२॥

Having said this, the Lord disappeared; and this Mārīṣā is the wife of you ten, O princes.

VP 1.15.73

ततः सोमस्य वचनाज् जगृहुस् ते प्रचेतसः ।

Then, at Soma’s word, the ten Pracetases accepted Mārīṣā, restraining their anger toward the trees, and fulfilled the duty of husbands as Prajāpatis.

From the ten Pracetases and Mārīṣā was born the illustrious Prajāpati Dakṣa, who had formerly arisen from Brahmā.

Śrīdhara translation

Dakṣa, who was formerly Brahmā’s son, was born from Māriṣā.

VP 1.15.75

स तु दक्षो महाभागः सृष्ट्यर्थं सुमहामते ।
पुत्रान् उत्पादयामास प्रजासृष्ट्यर्थम् आत्मनः ॥७५॥

That great Dakṣa produced sons for the creation of beings.

Śrīdhara translation

For creation, that is, for increasing the Prajāpati creation and for his own progeny, for continuation.

VP 1.15.76

अवरांश् च वरांश् च द्विपदोऽथ चतुष्पदान् ।
आदेशं ब्रह्मणः कुर्वन् सृष्ट्यर्थं समुपस्थितः ॥७६॥

He produced inferior and superior beings, bipeds and quadrupeds, carrying out Brahmā’s command and undertaking creation.

Śrīdhara translation

Avarā means low or inferior, varā means excellent; alternatively, the reading is “moving and unmoving.”

Śrīdhara · transliteration

Devanagari

VP 1.15.77

स सृष्ट्वा मनसा दक्षः पश्चाद् असृजत स्त्रियः ।
ददौ स दश धर्माय कश्यपाय त्रयोदश ।
कालस्य नयने युक्ताः सप्तविंशतिम् इन्दवे ॥७७॥

After creating them mentally, Dakṣa gave ten daughters to Dharma, thirteen to Kaśyapa, and twenty-seven, appointed to mark the passage of time, to the Moon.

Śrīdhara translation

After the destruction of his sons Haryaśvas and others, he created sixty daughters. He briefly introduces the marriages and lineages of fifty of them, with the two verses beginning “he gave.”

sa sṛṣṭvā manasā dakṣaḥ paścād asṛjata striyaḥ | dadau sa daśa dharmāya kaśyapāya trayodaśa | kālasya nayane yuktāḥ saptaviṃśatim indave || 77 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

paścāt haryaśvādiputranāśāntaraṃ striyaḥ kanyāḥ ṣaṣṭimasṛjat| tāsāṃ madhye paścāśat kanyānāṃ vivāhān vaṃśāṃś ca pradarśanārthamupakṣipati, dadāviti dvābhyām

Śrīdhara · Devanagari

पश्चात् हर्यश्वादिपुत्रनाशान्तरं स्त्रियः कन्याः षष्टिमसृजत्। तासां मध्ये पश्चाशत् कन्यानां विवाहान् वंशांश् च प्रदर्शनार्थमुपक्षिपति, ददाविति द्वाभ्याम्

VP 1.15.78

तासु देवास् तथा दैत्या नागा गावस् तथा खगाः ।
गन्धर्वाप्सरसश् चैव दानवाद्याश् च जज्ञिरे ॥७८॥

From them were born gods, Daityas, serpents, cows, birds, Gandharvas, Apsarases, Dānavas, and others.

Śrīdhara translation

Kṛttikā and the other constellations are appointed according to the movement and order of time. Hence the statement: Kṛttikā and the other lunar mansions are the Moon’s wives.

VP 1.15.79

ततःप्रभृति मैत्रेय प्रजा मैथुनसम्भवाः ।

From that time onward, O Maitreya, beings were born through sexual union; formerly, the offspring of those accomplished ascetics arose through resolve, sight, touch, and special austerities.

ततो दक्षात् प्रभृति

VP 1.15.80

अङ्गुष्ठाद् दक्षिणाद् दक्षः पूर्वं जातो मया श्रुतः ।
कथं प्राचेतसो भूयः समुत्पन्नो महामुने ॥८०॥

I have heard that Dakṣa was formerly born from Brahmā’s right thumb. How, then, O great sage, was he born again as the son of the Pracetases?

Śrīdhara translation

Before Dakṣa, beings arose merely from will and similar means. Although sexual creation did exist even before Dakṣa the Prācetasa, this is said with reference to its predominance; hence there is no contradiction.

VP 1.15.81

एष मे संशयो ब्रह्मन् महान् हृदि वर्तते ।
यद् दौहित्रश् च सोमस्य पुनः श्वशुरतां गतः ॥८१॥

This is my great doubt, O Brahman: how did he, who was Soma’s grandson, become Soma’s father-in-law again?

Śrīdhara translation

Since Māriṣā is Soma’s daughter, Dakṣa is Soma’s daughter’s son. And since it was said that Soma gave twenty-seven daughters to the Moon, he also became the father-in-law; this is the doubt. If the reading is “of the daughter’s son,” Soma was born from Atri in Dakṣa’s daughter Anasūyā, making Dakṣa his maternal grandfather. How could Dakṣa again become Soma’s father-in-law?

VP 1.15.82

उत्पत्तिश् च निरोधश् च नित्यो भूतेषु सर्वदा ।
ऋषयोऽत्र न मुह्यन्ति ये चान्ये दिव्यचक्षुषः ॥८२॥

Among beings, birth and dissolution are always occurring. Those who possess divine vision, including the sages, are not confused by this.

Śrīdhara translation

The two are eternal and uninterrupted in the form of a continuous stream.

नित्यौ प्रवाहरूपेणाविच्छिन्नौ

VP 1.15.83

युगे युगे भवन्त्य् एते दक्षाद्या मुनिसत्तम ।
पुनश् चैवं निरुद्ध्यन्ते विद्वांस् तत्र न मुह्यति ॥८३॥

These Dakṣas and others arise again in every age and are again absorbed; the wise are not bewildered by this.

Śrīdhara translation

By “Dakṣa and others,” the word “and others” includes Soma. They arise in every age and now appear like the Buddha; they are withdrawn like beings in deep sleep. An age is a period in which planets come together in one place; it may mean a kalpa, manvantara and so on. Dakṣa was born in the Svāyambhuva manvantara and again from the Pracetases in the Cākṣuṣa; Soma too was born from Atri and again from the ocean of milk. Thus there is no contradiction.

VP 1.15.84

कानिष्ठ्यं ज्यैष्ठम् अप्य् एषां पूर्वं नाभूद् द्विजोत्तम ।
तप एव गरीयोऽभूत् प्रभावश् चैव कारणम् ॥८४॥

Among them there was no fixed seniority or junior status, O excellent Brahman; austerity and spiritual power were the determining causes.

Śrīdhara translation

He preempts concern that a very junior father-in-law should be worshipped by saying “juniority”: greater austerity and power, not mere age, made him superior.

VP 1.15.85

देवानां दानवानां च गन्धर्वोरगरक्षसाम् ।
उत्पत्तिं विस्तरेणेह मम ब्रह्मन् प्रकीर्तय ॥८५॥

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

VP 1.15.93

बालिशा बत यूयं वै नास्या जानीत वै भुवः ।
अन्तर् ऊर्ध्वम् अधश् चैव कथं सृक्ष्यथ वै प्रजाः ॥९३॥

You seem to very childish, because you don't know what is there above or below, what is inside this earth; you don't know anything about this earth, how do you create?

Śrīdhara translation

The literal meaning of “Alas, you are childish” is: “Alas, you are ignorant, without discrimination, and will suffer the labor of creating beings without examining the truth. You do not know the lower beginning, upper end or middle of the subtle body, the field from which worldly existence sprouts. How will you create beings without knowing the path to liberation? Why create a worldly cycle in vain?”

Śrīdhara · Devanagari

VP 1.15.94

ऊर्ध्वं तिर्यग् अधश् चैव यदाप्रतिहता गतिः ।
तदा कस्माद् भुवो नान्तं सर्वे द्रक्ष्यथ बालिशाः ॥९४॥

When you can go, you are unstoppable, and you are capable of going above, below, everywhere, why are you not going to see the ends of the world? You seem to be now knowledgeable, you are childish.

Śrīdhara translation

Because you have pure intellect and are qualified for knowledge of reality, he says: you are able to consider what is above, across and below; if your understanding is now obstructed in the human birth, why will you not see the end of the subtle body, and strive for knowledge that ends its dissolution? As explained in the sixth Skandha in Nārada’s instruction to the Haryaśvas, the earth is the field and seed of beginningless bondage; without seeing its cessation, what use are unreal actions?

ūrdhvaṃ tiryag adhaś caiva yadāpratihatā gatiḥ | tadā kasmād bhuvo nāntaṃ sarve drakṣyatha bāliśāḥ || 94 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

yūyañca viśuddhabuddhitvāt tattvajñānādhikāriṇa evety āha—ūrdhvam iti| ūrdhvaṃ tiryagadhaś ca sarvatastattvavicāre yadā idānīṃ nṛjanmani yuṣmākaṃ gatirbuddhipratihatā varttate, tadā kasmād bhuvo liṅgaśarīrasyāntaṃ na drakṣyatha? liṅgabhaṅgaparyante tattvajñāne kim iti yatnaṃ ya kariṣyathety arthaḥ| taduktaṃ ṣaṣṭhaskandhe haryaśvairnāradoktivivaraṇe— bhūḥ kṣetraṃ bījasañjñaṃ yad anādinijabandhanam| adṛṣṭvā tasya nirvāṇaṃ kimasatkarmabhirbhavet?| ityādi

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

यूयञ्च विशुद्धबुद्धित्वात् तत्त्वज्ञानाधिकारिण एवेत्य् आह—ऊर्ध्वम् इति। ऊर्ध्वं तिर्यगधश् च सर्वतस्तत्त्वविचारे यदा इदानीं नृजन्मनि युष्माकं गतिर्बुद्धिप्रतिहता वर्त्तते, तदा कस्माद् भुवो लिङ्गशरीरस्यान्तं न द्रक्ष्यथ? लिङ्गभङ्गपर्यन्ते तत्त्वज्ञाने किम् इति यत्नं य करिष्यथेत्य् अर्थः। तदुक्तं षष्ठस्कन्धे हर्यश्वैर्नारदोक्तिविवरणे— भूः क्षेत्रं बीजसञ्ज्ञं यद् अनादिनिजबन्धनम्। अदृष्ट्वा तस्य निर्वाणं किमसत्कर्मभिर्भवेत्?। इत्य्आदि

VP 1.15.95

ते तु तद्वचनं श्रुत्वा प्रयाताः सर्वतो दिशम् ।
अद्यापि नो निवर्तन्ते समुद्रेभ्य इवापगाः ॥९५॥

They listened to Naarada's words, and thought that what Naarada is telling is right. We should go and see what is there above and below the earth, and find out and then only we should create. They went all around in all directions, and till now they have not returned, just like rivers which go and join the ocean, never come back.

Śrīdhara translation

They went in every direction, unattached, freely moving in all directions; because they were liberated, they have not returned even now.

VP 1.15.96

हर्यश्वेष्व् अथ नष्टेषु दक्षः प्राचेतसः पुनः ।

When the Haryashvaas were lost, Praachetasaas ' son Dakṣa, created another thousand children, in Vairuni.

Śrīdhara translation

After they had disappeared, wherever they had gone.

VP 1.15.97

विवर्धयिषवस् ते तु शबलाश्वाः प्रजाः पुनः ।
पूर्वोक्तं वचनं ब्रह्मन् नारदेनैव नोदिताः ॥९७॥

They were called Shabalaashvaas. Again Naarada approaches them and tells them the same thing - without knowing the end of the world, why are you engaged in this creation? You seem to be very childish. You are not knowledgeable.

No commentary.

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

VP 1.15.98

अन्योऽन्यम् ऊचुस् ते सर्वे सम्यग् आह महामुनिः ।
भ्रातृणाम् पदवी चैव गन्तव्या नात्र संशयः ॥९८॥

The Shabalaashvaas told each other that what Naarada is telling is correct, we should also follow our brothers, who went all around to know the ends of the earth. There is no doubt about this.

Śrīdhara translation

Having been urged and instructed by the earlier words “Alas, you are childish,” they said: the path of our elder brothers and teachers is the path of liberation.

VP 1.15.99

ज्ञात्वा प्रमाणं पृथ्व्याश् च प्रजाः स्रक्ष्यामहे ततः ।
तेऽपि तेनैव मार्गेण प्रयाताः सर्वतोमुखम् ।
अद्यापि न निवर्तन्ते समुद्रेभ्य इवापगाः ॥९९॥

Let us know the limits of the earth, and then let us create more people. They also went just like their brothers, all around in all directions. They also have not returned till now, just as the rivers which go and join the oceans never come back.

Śrīdhara translation

Knowing the extent of the earth, meaning the limit of the subtle body, we will afterwards create creatures to preserve the order, by obeying our father’s command; this is the literal narrative meaning.

VP 1.15.100

ततः प्रभृति वै भ्राता भ्रातुर् अन्वेषणे द्विज ।
प्रयातो नश्यति तथा तन् न कार्यं विजानता ॥१००॥

Parāśara is giving an advice here. From that time onwards, a person who goes searching after his lost brothers never returns. One who is knowledgeable should not go searching for a brother who is lost; he will also be lost.

Śrīdhara translation

He says “from then onward” with that intention: “perishes” means “goes out of sight.”

VP 1.15.101

तांश् चापि नष्टान् विज्ञाय पुत्रान् दक्षः प्रजापतिः ।
क्रोधं चक्रे महाभागो नारदं स शशाप च ॥१०१॥

Knowing that even Shabalaashvaas were lost just like Haryashvaas, Dakṣa Prajaapati became very angry with Naarada, and cursed him.

Śrīdhara translation

The curse of Nārada is also stated in the Bhāgavata: “Therefore, fools, you shall have no place in the worlds.”

VP 1.15.102

सर्गकामस् ततो विद्वान् स मैत्रेय प्रजापतिः ।

Desiring to create, Dakṣa, it is said, created sixty daughters from Vairuṇī.

VP 1.15.103

ददौ स दश धर्माय कश्यपाय त्रयोदश ।
सप्तविंशति सोमाय चतस्रोऽरिष्टनेमिने ॥१०३॥

Out of these 60 daughters, he gave 10 daughters in marriage to Dharma, and 13 daughters to Kaashyapa. He gave 27 daughters in marriage to Soma, and 4 daughters to Arishtanemi.

Śrīdhara translation

He begins to narrate the lineages of the daughters with “he gave”; “twenty-seven” is an archaic omission of the accusative ending.

द्वे चैव बहुपुत्राय द्वे चैवाङ्गिरसे तथा ।
द्वे कृशाश्वाय विदुषे तासां नामानि मे शृणु ॥१०४॥

VP 1.15.105

अरुन्धती वसुर् यामिर् लम्बा भानुर् मरुत्वती ।
सङ्कल्पा च मुहूर्ता च साध्या विश्वा च तादृशी ।
धर्मपत्न्यो दश त्व् एतास् तास्व् अपत्यानि मे शृणु ॥१०५॥

Dharma's wives names are being told here. He gave 10 daughters, Arundathi, Vasu, Jamee, Langhaa, Bhaanu, Marudvati, Sankalpaa, Muhoortaa, Saadhyaa and Vishvaa. The children which they had - listen to me.

No commentary.

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

अरुन्धती वसुर् यामिर् लम्बा भानुर् मरुत्वती ।
सङ्कल्पा च मुहूर्ता च साध्या विश्वा च तादृशी ।
धर्मपत्न्यो दश त्व् एतास् तास्व् अपत्यानि मे शृणु ॥१०५॥

VP 1.15.106

विश्वेदेवास् तु विश्वायाः साध्या साध्यान् अजायत ।
मरुत्वत्यां मरुत्वन्तो वसोश् च वसवः स्मृताः ।
भानोस् तु भानवः पुत्रा मुहूर्तायां मुहूर्तजाः ॥१०६॥

Vishvaa's sons were called as Vishve Devaas. Saadhyaa gave birth to Saadhya Devaas, called Saadhyaas. Marudvati g ave birth to Marudvaans. Vasu's children were called Vasus - ashta Vasus. Muhoortaa gave birth to Muhoortaas. Bhaanu gave birth to Bhaanus, Aadityaas.

Śrīdhara translation

The thirteen wives of Dharma mentioned earlier—beginning with Śraddhā—are daughters of the Svāyambhuva Dakṣa. The ten beginning with Arundhatī belong to the Prācetasa Dakṣa; thus there is no contradiction.

Śrīdhara · transliteration

pūrvoktāḥ śrāddhādyāstrayodaśa dharmasya patnyaḥ svāyambhuvasya dakṣasya kanyāḥ| etāstvarūndhatyādyā daśa prācetasasya dakṣasyetyavirodhaḥ

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

VP 1.15.107

लम्बायाश् चैव घोषोऽथ नागवीथी तु यामिजा ॥१०७॥

Langhaa's son was Ghosha, Jaamee gave birth to Naagaveethi.

Śrīdhara translation

Muhūrta-born are the gods presiding over each muhūrta; Ghoṣa is the presiding deity of that period; Nāgavīthī is the deity presiding over the northern celestial path of the gods.

VP 1.15.108

पृथिवीविषयं सर्वम् अरुन्धत्याम् अजायत ।
सङ्कल्पायास् तु सर्वात्मा जज्ञे सङ्कल्प एव हि ॥१०८॥

Everything related to Pruthivi was born from Arundathi. From Sankalpaa was born the one who controls everything, the Sankalpa itself.

Śrīdhara translation

The whole realm whose location is the earth, consisting of all moving and unmoving beings; “all-self” means having every object as its field.

VP 1.15.109

ये त्व् अनेकवसुप्राणदेवा ज्योतिःपुरोगमाः ।
वसवोऽष्टौ समाख्यातास् तेषां वक्ष्यामि विस्तरम् ॥१०९॥

They are all children of Dharma through his 10 wives. Those whose strength are the wealth, powerful rays, they are called as Vasus, I am going to tell you about them.

No commentary.

Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

VP 1.15.110

आपो ध्रुवश् च सोमश् च धर्मश् चैवानिलोऽनलः ।
प्रत्यूषश् च प्रभासश् च वसवो नामभिः स्मृताः ॥११०॥

Their names are Aapa, Dhruva, Soma, Dharma, Anila, Anala, Pratyoosha, Prabhaasa.

Śrīdhara translation

He begins the lineages of the eight Vasus by their names, through “Viśvarūpa, greatly renowned.” “Many” means various; “those whose vital strength is that very radiance are the many-Vasu-prāṇas.” This explains the etymology of the Vasus; scripture says, “I am the fire among the Vasus.”

Śrīdhara · Devanagari

VP 1.15.111

आपस्य पुत्रो वैतण्डः श्रमः शान्तो ध्वनिस् तथा ।
ध्रुवस्य पुत्रो भगवान् कालो लोकप्रकालनः ॥१११॥

Aapa had 4 children, Stabdha, Shrama, Shaanta, Dhuni. Dhruva's son was Kaala.

Śrīdhara translation

Āpa had four sons, including Vaitandya; Lokaprakālana is their destroyer.

VP 1.15.112

Soma's son was Varchas, and because of him one becomes a Varchasvi, one who has an aura around him.

VP 1.15.113

धर्मस्य पुत्रो द्रविणो हुतहव्यवहस् तथा ।
मनोहरायां शिशिरः प्राणोऽथ वरुणस् तथा ॥११३॥

Dharma through his wife Manoharaa, had Dravina, Hutahavyava, Shishira, Praana and Ramana. There is one more way of splitting - Huta and Havyavah.

No commentary.

VP 1.15.114

अनिलस्य शिवा भार्या तस्याः पुत्रो मनोजवः ।
अविज्ञातगतिश् चैव द्वौ पुत्राव् अनिलस्य तु ॥११४॥

Anila's wife was Śivaa, and her sons were Purojava and Avijnyaatagati.

Śrīdhara translation

From whom a radiant man is born; Dhara’s wife was Manoharā, and their five sons included Draviṇa. He is called Vahni because he carries the oblation.

VP 1.15.115

अग्निपुत्रः कुमारस् तु शरस्तम्बे व्यजायत ।
तस्य शाखो विशाखश् च नैगमेयश् च पृष्ठजाः ॥११५॥

Agni Putra was Agni's son earlier, he was born in Shatastambha. Shaakha, Vishaakha, Naigameya are the younger brothers of Kumaara. Or also, Agni's son is Kumaara, Naigameya was born of the back portion of Kumaara.

Śrīdhara translation

The arrow was made from the stalk of the muñja grass.

शरो मुञ्जस्तस्य स्तम्बे

VP 1.15.116

अपत्यं कृत्तिकानां तु कार्तिकेय इति स्मृतः ॥११६॥

Kruttikaa's son is known to be Kaartikeya.

Śrīdhara translation

Born from the back means younger sons, a group in the form of Kumāras. The Śalya-parvan says the Lord Guha became four-formed in an instant; hence Kumāra is identified with Kārttikeya.

VP 1.15.118

बृहस्पतेस् तु भगिनी वरस्त्री ब्रह्मचारिणी ।
योगसिद्धा जगत्कृत्स्नम् असक्ता विचरत्य् उत ।
प्रभासस्य तु सा भार्या वसूनाम् अष्टमस्य तु ॥११८॥

Bruhaspati had a sister, called Varastree, a Brahmachaarini, she had a lot of yogic powers. She was going around in the world with a lot of virakti, being dispassionate with everything. The eighth Vasu is Prabhaasa. Varastree became the wife of Prabhaasa.

Śrīdhara translation

She was the sister of Bṛhaspati and wife of Prabhāsa; “asaktā” means free from attachment.

VP 1.15.122

VP 1.15.124

कश्यपस्य तु भार्या यास् तासां नामानि मे शृणु ।
अदितिर् दितिर् दनुश् चैवारिष्टा च सुरसा स्वसा ॥१२४॥

These Rudraas had great ojas; they are told as hundred also, and one also. Now, listen to the names of the wives of Kaashyapa. Aditi, Diti, Danu, Arishtaa, Surasaa, Kashaa,...

Śrīdhara translation

Alternatively, this means the infinity of these manifestations, since śata can mean innumerable; scripture says there are countless thousands of Rudras on earth. The lineages of Dharma’s wives have now been given. He now begins the lineages of the thirteen given to Kaśyapa, beginning “Aditi…”

सुरभिर् विनता चैव ताम्रा क्रोधवशा इरा ।
कद्रुर् मुनिश् च धर्मज्ञ तदपत्यानि मे शृणु ॥१२५॥

VP 1.15.126

पूर्वमन्वन्तरे श्रेष्ठा द्वादशासन् सुरोत्तमाः ।
तुषिता नाम तेऽन्योऽन्यम् ऊचुर् वैवस्वतेऽन्तरे ॥१२६॥

In the earlier Manvantara, there were 12 gods, they were called Tushitaas, group of gods. In Vaivasvata Manvantara, they met and discussed like this.

Śrīdhara translation

He says in story form that the Tuṣitas were born as Aditi’s sons. In the former Cākṣuṣa manvantara there were gods called Tuṣitas; when the Vaivasvata manvantara approached, they assembled and said to one another…

VP 1.15.127

उपस्थितेऽतियशसश् चाक्षुषस्यान्तरे मनोः ।
समवायीकृताः सर्वे समागम्य परस्परम् ॥१२७॥

They came together in the Chaakshusha Manvantara, which is the 6th Manvantara, and spoke to each other like this.

Śrīdhara translation

Of great renown, famous for their power.

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

अतियशसः प्रख्यातवीर्याः

VP 1.15.128

आगच्छत द्रुतं देवा अदितिं सम्प्रविश्य वै ।
मन्वन्तरे प्रसूयामस् तन् नः श्रेयो भवेद् इति ॥१२८॥

They all mutually had a conversation that let us all get into the womb of Aditi, and be born in the Vaivasvata Manvantara, the seventh Manvantara, and it will do all good for us.

Śrīdhara translation

He says what they said: “Come”; let us be born in the Vaivasvata manvantara. Why? Because by regaining the office of gods, our welfare will again arise.

VP 1.15.129

एवम् उक्त्वा तु ते सर्वे चाक्षुषस्यान्तरे मनोः ।
मारीचात् कश्यपाज् जाता अदित्या दक्षकन्यया ॥१२९॥

Having spoken thus in Chaakshusha Manvantara, Kashyapa who is the son of Mareecha, and Kashyapa's wife Aditi, who is daughter of Dakṣa, through her, they were born.

No commentary.

Then, Viṣṇu and Indra were again born. In the same way, Aryamaa, Dhaataa, Tvashtru, Pooshaa, were also born.

No commentary.

VP 1.15.131

विवस्वान् सविता चैव मित्रो वरुण एव च ।
अंशुर् भगश् चातितेजा आदित्या द्वादश स्मृताः ॥१३१॥

Also Vivasvaan, Savitaa, Mitra, Varuṇa, were also born. Amshu, Bhaga, were all the 12 Aadityaas, who had great tejas. They were the Dvaadasha Aadityaas.

Śrīdhara translation

They were born from Kaśyapa through Aditi as the instrumental mother in the Vaivasvata manvantara, although they had spoken these words in the Cākṣuṣa manvantara.

Śrīdhara · Devanagari

चाक्षुषस्य मनोरन्तरे एवम् उक्त्वा वैवस्वतमन्वन्तरे अदित्या निमित्तभूतया कश्यपाज्जाता इत्य् अर्थः

VP 1.15.132

चाक्षुषस्यान्तरे पूर्वम् आसन् ये तुषिताः सुराः ।
वैवस्वतेऽन्तरे ते वै आदित्या द्वादश स्मृताः ॥१३२॥

Those gods known as Tushitaas in Chaakshusha Manvantara, in the Vaivasvata Manvantara, they only came to be known as Dvaadasha Aadityaas.

Śrīdhara translation

Thus he concludes that the twelve Ādityas are the Tuṣitas themselves.

cākṣuṣasyāntare pūrvam āsan ye tuṣitāḥ surāḥ | vaivasvate’ntare te vai ādityā dvādaśa smṛtāḥ || 132 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

atastuṣitā eva dvādaśādityāḥ smṛtā ityupasaṃharati

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

अतस्तुषिता एव द्वादशादित्याः स्मृता इत्युपसंहरति

VP 1.15.133

याः सप्तविंशतिः प्रोक्ताः सोमपत्न्योऽथ सुव्रताः ।
सर्वा नक्षत्रयोगिन्यस् तन्नाम्न्यश् चैव ताः स्मृताः ॥१३३॥

It was told that Dakṣa gave 27 daughters to Soma. And they were all associated with Nakshatraas, Ashvini, Bharani, etc. They also had a lot of children who had a lot of tejas.

Śrīdhara translation

So far, following the scriptural sequence of eight Vasus, eleven Rudras and twelve Ādityas, Aditi’s lineage has been given. Now, although Diti’s lineage comes next by the order of Kaśyapa’s wives, it is postponed because Prahlāda’s story is lengthy; he first gives the shorter lineages of the other daughters, beginning “the twenty-seven who were mentioned,” in five and a half verses.

VP 1.15.134

तासाम् अपत्यान्यभवन् दीप्तान्यमिततेजसाम् ।
अरिष्टनेमिपत्नीनाम् अपत्यानीह षोडश ॥१३४॥

Now, Arishtanemi's 16 children, through his wives, I am going to tell you that.

Śrīdhara translation

Their offspring became radiant through their extraordinary splendour; according to another reading, they became bright because of immeasurable radiance. He now gives the lineage of Ariṣṭanemi, one of the four mentioned.

tāsām apatyānyabhavan dīptānyamitatejasām | ariṣṭanemipatnīnām apatyānīha ṣoḍaśa || 134 ||

Śrīdhara · transliteration

Devanagari
Śrīdhara · Devanagari

तासामतितेजसा दीप्तिमन्त्यपत्यान्भवन्। पाठान्तरे अमिततेजसो हेतोर्दीप्ता न्यभवन्नित्य् अर्थः। चतस्रोऽरिष्टनेमय इत्य् उक्तानां वंशम् आह—अरिष्ट नेमि इति

VP 1.15.135

बहुपुत्रस्य विदुषश् चतस्रो विद्युतः स्मृताः ॥१३५॥

The knowledgeable Bahuputra, his four Vidyuts are known. Whether they are children or wives, different commentaries tell differently.

No commentary.

Viṣṇu Purāṇa · transliteration

bahuputrasya viduṣaś catasro vidyutaḥ smṛtāḥ || 135 ||

Devanagari
Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

बहुपुत्रस्य विदुषश् चतस्रो विद्युतः स्मृताः ॥१३५॥

VP 1.15.136

प्रत्यङ्गिरसजाः श्रेष्ठा ऋचो ब्रह्मर्षिसत्कृताः ।
कृशाश्वस्य तु देवर्षेर् देवप्रहरणाः स्मृताः ॥१३६॥

Now, born of Pratyangiras, the Rig mantraas, which are shreshta mantraas, well honoured, respected by Brahmaṛṣis, are told.

Śrīdhara translation

He gives the lineage of the two mentioned under Bahuputra. The four lightning forms are known in astrology: Vātā, Kapilā, Atīlohitā and Pītā; white is associated with drought. He then gives the lineage of the two sons of Aṅgiras: the Pratyaṅgirasas, born from his return; they are deities presiding over thirty-five mantras.

At the end of a thousand yugas all these classes of gods are born again; O child, thirty-three are born from chandas.

He gives the lineage of the two sons of Kṛśāśva: the gods who are divine weapons.

VP 1.15.138

तेषाम् अपीह सततं निरोधोत्पत्तिर् उच्यते ॥१३८॥

At the end of the yuga, they are again born. All the deva ganaas told - 33 in number, Dvaadashaadityaas, Ekaadasha Rudraas, Ashta Vasus, Ashvini Devataas are told in one place, Indra and Prajaapati are told in another place; out of their own willing, they are born again and again, at the end of thousand yugaas, all the deva ganaas. Out of this, 33 are born of their own will. Their creation and destruction is also told - all the time it happens repeatedly.

Śrīdhara translation

The thirty-three gods, eight Vasus, eleven Rudras, twelve Ādityas, Prajāpati and Vaṣaṭkāra are taught in scripture. “Born of will” means born by free choice; “creation with withdrawal” means their arising includes their subsequent withdrawal.

VP 1.15.139

यथा सूर्यस्य मैत्रेय उदयास्तमनाव् इह ।
एवं देवनिकायास् ते सम्भवन्ति युगे युगे ॥१३९॥

Just like the rising and setting of the Sun happens everyday, in the same way, the host of devaas are also created again and again in every yuga.

Śrīdhara translation

If creation and withdrawal involve desire, how can that be? He answers: just as the sun does it—in each age, in every kalpa, manvantara and so on.

VP 1.15.140

दित्याः पुत्रद्वयं जज्ञे कश्यपाद् इति नः श्रुतम् ।

PI have heard that Diti had two sons from Kaashyapa. They were Hiranyakashipu and Hiranyaaksha whom nobody can win over in war.

He now begins Diti’s lineage with “of Diti,” continuing until the statement of Danu’s lineage in chapter 21.

VP 1.15.144

दैत्येन्द्रदीपितो वह्निः सर्वाङ्गोपचितो द्विज ।
न ददाह च यं विप्र वासुदेवे हृदि स्थिते ॥१४४॥

Prahlaada was so much intent on meditation on Vāsudeva, his mind was completely filled with the thought of Vāsudeva. Even the king of the demons, Hiranyakashipu, though he had put him in fire, and the fire had engulfed his entire body, it never burnt even a small part of his body.

No commentary.

VP 1.15.145

महार्णवान्तःसलिले स्थितस्य चलतो मही ।
चचाल सकला यस्य पाशबद्धस्य धीमतः ॥१४५॥

Prahlaada was tied with ropes, and thrown into the great ocean, under the waters. When he was immersed like that, being tied with ropes, he moved a little bit, and the entire world was shaken.

Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

VP 1.15.146

न भिन्नं विविधैः शस्त्रैर् यस्य दैत्येन्द्रपातितैः ।
शरीरमद्रिकठिनं सर्वत्राच्युतचेतसः ॥१४६॥

Because of his deep devotion of Achyuta, his entire body and mind were filled with the thought of Achyuta, his body had become so hard, as a great mountain. Even though he was pierced with a lot of sharp weapons, they could not pierce his body, as it had become so hard, just like mountain.

No commentary.

Devanagari

VP 1.15.147

विषाणलोज्ज्वलमुखा यस्य दैत्यप्रचोदिताः ।
नान्ताय सर्पपतयो बभूवुर् उरुतेजसः ॥१४७॥

All the kings of serpents, throwing fire-like venom, being commanded by Hiranyakashipu, they could never do anything to Prahlaada.

Śrīdhara translation

To grant embodied beings single-minded devotion to Narasiṃha like Prahlāda, he described his power, arousing eagerness to hear, beginning with “among them” and continuing to the end.

VP 1.15.148

शैलैर् आक्रान्तदेहोऽपि यः स्मरन् पुरुषोत्तमम् ।

He was hit with rocks, because he was thinking of Purushottama, being protected by the shield of the thought of Viṣṇu, he was still very much alive.

Śrīdhara translation

He was armed, made ready by remembrance of Viṣṇu.

VP 1.15.149

पतन्तम् उच्चाद् अवनिर् यम् उपेत्य महामतिम् ।
दधार दैत्यपतिना क्षिप्तं स्वर्गनिवासिना ॥१४९॥

The king of demons, Hiranyakashipu, living in swarga, threw him from the top of the mountains, on to the earth. Though he was falling like that, the goddess Pruthivi Devi, held him and supported him; nothing happened to him.

VP 1.15.150

यस्य संशोषको वायुर् देहे दैत्येन्द्रयोजितः ।
अवाप सङ्क्षयं सद्यश् चित्तस्थे मधुसूदने ॥१५०॥

The winds which dry up anything, though commanded by Hiranyakashipu, could not do anything, and the Vaayu itself got dried up immediately, because Madhusoodana was in the thought of Prahlaada.

VP 1.15.151

विषाणभङ्गम् उन्मत्ता मदहानिं च दिग्गजाः ।

The diggajaas were commanded to kill him with their huge and hard tusks, and they started to pierce his chest with their tusks. Their tusks themselves got broken, and all their energy got dried up. They could not do anything.

VP 1.15.153

शम्बरस्य च मायानां सहस्रमतिमायिनः ।
यस्मिन् प्रयुक्तं चक्रेण कृष्णस्य वितथीकृतम् ॥१५३॥

He called Shambaraasura and asked him to use his maayaa shakti; he created thousands of wonderful illusions to hurt Prahlaada, but Krishna's Sudarshana Chakra came and destroyed all the maayaas of Shambaraasura, and they all became futile. Shambaraasura could not touch him with his maayaa.

Śrīdhara translation

The Daitya king made them ready to strike with their tusks, training them in the manner of elephant instruction.

VP 1.15.154

दैत्येन्द्रसूदोपहृतं यस् तु हालाहलं विषम् ।
जारयामास मतिमान् अविकारम् अमत्सरी ॥१५४॥

He had no jealousy, and was the one endowed with great knowledge because of his deep meditation on Viṣṇu. The attendants of Hiranyakashipu gave him haalaahala poison, very powerful poison; Prahlaada digested all of them, without any vikaara. He had no reaction, and he simply digested the poison.

No commentary.

Viṣṇu Purāṇa · Devanagari

VP 1.15.155

समचेता जगत्यस्मिन् यः सर्वेष्व् एव जन्तुषु ।
यथात्मनि तथान्यत्र परं मैत्रगुणान्वितः ॥१५५॥

In respect of all beings, he was equally positioned, in this world. With respect to others, he was disposed as though towards himself. He was very friendly with respect to everyone.

Śrīdhara translation

The poison Hālāhala was brought and given by the Daitya king’s cooks. Hālāhalā is a terrible river in the Himalaya; the poison produced on its bank is called hālāhala. He digested it so that no change—gooseflesh, sweat, dryness of the throat and such symptoms—arose.

VP 1.15.156

धर्मात्मा सत्यशौचादिगुणानाम् आकरस् तथा ।
उपमानम् अशेषाणां साधूनां यः सदाभवत् ॥१५६॥

He was a dharmaatmaa, always following the path of dharma. He was the abode of all excellent qualities, satya, shoucha. He was upamaana for all the saadhus, all the time. All the saadhus were compared only with Prahlaada.
